That means that this issue is more extensive, it is more about this data exchange, but it has been started yesterday on some important points, as I think, the right measures.

This is where the Constitutional Service has a clear attitude. I never rule out the fact that there is also a lawyer on a subject which is different, but there the Constitutional Service has a clear and, as I think, a very comprehensible attitude, saying: It is an equal treatment of foreign accounts. If someone moves and lives with us and therefore no longer has a foreign account, but an account of an Austrian citizen, then he will fall into Austrian banking secrecy anyway. But if you live abroad, then all foreigners, no matter where they are, are treated equally. This seems to me to be legally comprehensible and correct, and the Constitutional Service is here to express the greatest possible security of this legal position. That's the answer. The other, to the question of where to send the data and to whom, if in Austria there is a banking secret comparable to the German one, when our present is changed, is: You decide. And since I do not assume that the Austrian banking secret is changing, for me I do not ask this question of follow-up.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-05-23-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00204_d7e18085	Mr President. The debate we will be holding in the next hour is about how you really feel about direct democracy. This question would actually be addressed to the Minister for the Interior, but I am not at all unhappy that she is represented by you, Mr State Secretary, in short, because, among other things, you are one of those, if not even the main person in the ÖVP, who – at least in the announcements – demands direct democracy. You made very concrete proposals a year ago. It is therefore also about how seriously you mean it. What did this request deal with? – With the European Citizens’ Initiative. We introduced these into the EU a year ago, the first direct democratic instrument available to the European people. It has been applicable since 1. ==References== Already in the implementation negotiations here in the House, we have pointed out greens, but also others, to various problems, for example, the problem of Austria's capacity to introduce particularly high obstacles to the support of this European citizens' initiative. The support expressions can be collected on the street or online. In Austria, unlike other countries, one needs either a passport or a identity card in order to support a European citizens' initiative. I – like other critics – assumed that there are probably Austrians who would be entitled to support such a European citizens' initiative because of the right to vote, but who may not have a passport or a identity card because they do not need both of them – for example because they carry a driver's license as a photo identification and for various reasons no longer or no longer travel. I have asked the Minister for the Interior whether she can call us a number of how many people would be affected, so how many people are excluded from the possibility of exercising their democratic right. The Minister's reply at the time was that she could not say that, unfortunately, because the two data sets are not compatible, she assumes that there are a significant number of people who have neither a passport nor a identity card. At the time, therefore, we have already pointed out that this high obstacle actually leads to the fact that some people cannot make use of their democratic right, and then, in longer negotiations among the parties, have agreed that at least in the form of a resolution, we ensure that the Ministry of the Interior specifically checks whether there is no other means of legitimacy or identification than the passport and the identity card. This motion for a resolution states that the Minister is invited by Parliament, as soon as possible but at the latest on the basis of the evaluation in three years' time for the amendment of the ECI Regulation at European level, to provide the Commission with a central online collection system and to receive a uniform cost replacement from European citizens' initiatives. The Minister was also invited to examine the conditions under which the list of personal identification documents – currently passport and identity card – could be extended in order to facilitate the support of as many people as possible, and, where appropriate, to make a change to the European Commission on the basis of the outcome of this review. So, on the one hand, the issue was the ID papers and on the other hand, which system is used to carry out this online collection, and which costs are associated with them. This is why we have advocated that there is a common, central online system, so that all possible initiatives can be taken to preserve water resources or other issues. In this context, the Commission will be able to take the necessary steps to reduce the costs of the various initiatives. These were the two parts of this motion for a resolution. One year after the entry into force of this European Citizens' Initiative, I asked what was implemented. The Minister's reply in March 2013 was: "The Federal Electoral Authority has not been submitted to the European Citizens' Initiatives in paper form or in electronic form for review. The collection of relevant experiences with the review of support terms seems to me to be one of the basic prerequisites to be in the sense of the resolution of the National Council of 29 December. ==References== 231/E). In the context of Greece's rescue and rehabilitation, Greece itself must make a high contribution. In fact, part of the discussion was whether the path was absolutely the right one, or whether one could have taken a more balanced, investment-oriented path and thus not perhaps the social price would have been lower. But no matter how one now evaluates this question: At the time, a set of measures has been established by the international community of States with Greece, i.e. this train-train principle, whenever measures are implemented in Greece, there are also rescue and support measures. In the course of this package, it has also been stated that the European Union, the euro-states also buy securities, namely, buy Greek securities, not as an investment, because they have seen this as a good investment, but within the framework of this Securities Market Programme, that is, precisely within the framework of the rescue programme. Only by buying these Greek securities has the value of these securities increased again, which was also the meaning and idea of stabilisation, just as there are interest incomes. It has already been stated that, in the course of this programme, it is useful to stabilise these gains or increases in value, which have resulted in the balance sheets of the central banks – which were not part of the idea itself, because part of the idea of stabilizing Greece as a whole, so that the euro can also be stabilised and thus also stabilised the international trade and financial relations within the euro area – and to reinvent it again in Greece, since it is still more sensible to recover these profits than perhaps to link another rescue package in the future. It is therefore not the fourth rescue package, but it has been a completed part of the then Greece's package of measures. It is important to stress that it is reviewed half-yearly, by the troika. And only if the Troika issue a positive test certificate and Greece fulfils its measures will it be re-founded. We have to decide today, because this is actually from the balance sheet of the central bank, but of course also needs a legal basis. One of the other points I would like to address briefly is also an increase in the International Monetary Fund. I also think this makes sense because the International Monetary Fund has shown itself to be extremely efficient and extremely helpful and sometimes even the only useful instrument in the global financial landscape, especially in the global financial crisis fight. It is therefore also useful to provide there the firepower, the capacity to enable what it should do. One point which has not yet been mentioned in the discussion, but which I think is very important, is an investment protection agreement in this case with Nigeria. This is important, therefore, because it is a question of protecting the investments of those companies from Austria who want to invest abroad – in that case in Nigeria – as well. These are precisely those agreements that also bring more fairness and more justice into international trade relations. Nigeria is not one of Austria's most important foreign trade partners in sub-Saharan Africa after South Africa, and is one of the fastest growing economies in the world. And it would hardly be believed, because the Nigerian economy actually lives mainly from the export of crude oil, but growth has also been driven by the non-petroleum sector, namely services, the construction sector, banks, trade and tourism. Austrian trade with Nigeria includes machines and Vorarlberger tops, i.e. fabrics from Vorarlberg, which also makes it clear how small the global village has become, as the future prospects of the Vorarlberg textile industry depend on such decisions here. This may not have been so highlighted in the discussion, but, I believe, it is of great importance for the economic well-being of individual regions in Austria. That is why I hope that all these laws, including those last mentioned, will be approved.

I think that this bilateral investment protection agreement with Nigeria is a good reason to talk again about how far it would be possible to change our Austrian model investment protection agreement – of course, knowing that since the entry into force of the Treaty of Lisbon, most investment protection agreements are no longer bilateral, but rather multilateral, which are negotiated at EU level, where Austria – see, for example, the current agreement with Peru and Colombia, for example, or what is at the very beginning of the pipeline, i.e. with the USA – is implementing only more small parts of it, as well as other national states, but the large lion share is in the future with the EU. But you can see, in the example of Nigeria, that, despite the Lisbon Treaty, it is still possible, where there is no multilateral agreement – if I want to get a little bit sharper, I like to do so! – that it is necessary to do something on both levels, both bilateral and multilateral. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Gritish]] [[Abg. Grill:... In our model agreement on these two factors of labour rights and the environment, we have the formulation that it is not acceptable to amend environmental laws or employee protection laws, to levelle down, to generate investment or to make investment possible. . I am convinced that it is possible to find a much better, more gripping, stronger concept than this statehood and, of course, to respect the sovereignty of the states with which we conclude treaties. In conclusion, such investment protection agreements are important – equipped with transparent arbitration procedures – if they are good, because they really contribute to a coherent development policy, and I very much hope that this concrete agreement for Nigeria fulfils what Nigeria is hoping for, namely jobs, economic growth and, ultimately, the fight against poverty. – Thank you very much.

That would be all right if that didn't have side effects that I call extremely undesirable. The ECB, which is a central bank, is increasingly acting as a commercial bank by buying bonds in bond trading. But with this, she intervenes massively in the money cycles. This, in turn, leads to a completely distorted interest rate formation in the markets, which is in favour of the States and their refinancing, but which is likely to result in a fair assessment of the bonds. Rash bond by 5 percent yield, that can't be healthy! This leads to new bladder formations. [[Abg. May. Stefan: And what is the consequence of this?]] That is why our policy, Mr State Secretary, should be particularly critical of the low interest rate policy of the ECB. This product piracy law, however, is as a legal entity, I would say, rather without teeth. If you read the report carefully, you will see that this is not an official offence. If the customs authority has the suspicion that it is a forgery, and the notifier, represented by his legal representative, confirms this forgery, then there are three possibilities. He can say whether it is about it, the goods are still released, or one can seek an extermination, that is, the goods are actually destroyed, but one needs the consent of the one who has the goods, or the third, one goes to court and then there is a civil procedure. This is the case in the Product Piracy Act. But that's so complicated. If this is not the case, this is a punishment. This is an important measure for this group of persons, so that it knows what is paid for, what it can call for, so that it knows the basis of the negotiation. And this is also an important innovation, as precisely in these cases, in these events very many women or also men are ashamed of that, so to speak, according to the crime. It often takes a certain time for them to find the courage to go to the equal treatment prosecution in this case. The same applies to the Minister of Social Affairs. This too has already been done in the past, but we have now incorporated it into this law. Finally, let me make a comment on the so-called levelling-up: The issue is that no one should be discriminated against due to sex, due to age, because of sexual orientation. We would have liked to see something done together with this novel. Unfortunately, this has not succeeded. This is not such a novum in our law or in different laws, for example, if I look at the trade order. No one, for example, should be discriminated against because of the ethnicity or race. That the so-called levelling-up, namely the protection of discrimination for all groups – whether it be gay or lesbian, whether it is discrimination for age reasons – has not been achieved, I regret. We will, of course, continue to try to bring the federal law to the fore, on which the other eight federal states are also. It is only a federal country that has not passed any relevant laws, namely Lower Austria. All other federal states, for example, have laid down such provisions in their housing directives. Today we are adopting a reform, a renewal that is necessary. I'm glad about it, the next steps will surely follow again. Thank you very much.

I believe that we must do everything we can to help those concerned and not cut them off through a short period of time. We are very clearly committed to equal treatment and to the necessary measures, ladies and gentlemen, but I would like to address the levelling up very specifically. I would also like to say the following: Even if false things are said over and over again, this does not make them more correct, ladies and gentlemen. The fact is, there is a single country across the EU that has implemented all the discrimination features in the levelling-up, that is Great Britain, ladies and gentlemen. The 17 countries that have been mentioned on several occasions today – the Federal Minister has once spoken of 21 – have only implemented individual discrimination features outside the working world in accessing goods and services. So it has only one country implemented all the features, and exactly this country, Great Britain, is now fighting with a flood of complaints, in which it becomes really obvious what levelling-up means in concrete implementation and how difficult that is. Mrs Wurm, on the subject of countries – the Federal Minister has referred to it – I would like to say to you the following: Especially in the example of housing, which you have also mentioned, it is not about private homes, but about apartments for which the public service is responsible. It is also openly acknowledged that there are other possibilities here and that this is not an intervention in ownership and privacy. Where the public hand is responsible, one can also intervene accordingly, it must also do so and that makes sense. Otherwise, the country regulations are relatively toothless. If you are concerned about what that means in the federal states, you can see that this has quite a different quality and is much more extensive than it would be the levelling-up at the federal level in the federal law. One more thing about the EU Directive: The fact is, even if the wrong thing is not more correct, if one repeats it fifty times, that there is no directive adopted, that was also said by the Federal Minister in the Committee. We have the Equal Treatment Directive 2006, which was the last one to be adopted at EU level. We have put this into practice, ladies and gentlemen. That's what we're confessing. Since 2008, however, the directive, which contains the levelling-up, has been on ice, precisely because many other countries – not only Austria but many countries – have substantial problems because they have very specific concerns. These concerns, ladies and gentlemen, exist for good reason, because they can still be made small, ridiculous, ridiculed, as some of them have happened in committee. Levelling-up is a far-reaching intervention in privacy, in ownership, in private rights. Now I know that there are factions here in the house that concern private property, property or framework conditions for the economy only, but that is a central concern, ladies and gentlemen. It is always necessary to consider, if necessary, the need to avert and prevent discrimination, how far it allows a massive interference in ownership, in privacy, in the economy. To find this balance is our task and our responsibility, because anti-discrimination must not be the task of freedoms. I think that's a central issue. To the end, my Ladies and gentlemen, in order to reverse the burden of proof – the Green motion calls for it. We have already discussed this in committee. The conversion of the burden of proof would be a breach of our rule of law. According to this one, it is so that someone is innocent until the evidence of the opposite. In the case of a return to evidence, especially in the case of complaints, which are particularly relevant to the request, it may be that someone can claim – I say it deliberately placidly now – that he has been harassed and is therefore guilty until the evidence of the opposite. I believe, ladies and gentlemen, that is not unproblematic. Here we have to look very carefully at the instruments we really need when it comes to protecting against discrimination and harassment. I dare doubt that this is the turn of evidence. - Thank you.

Our opinion is: Yeah, prime sports make sense. This does not mean that one does not promote the rest, but that means that there can be a stronger form of focus in these sports. This is not included in the law now. It is true that the structures build very strongly on the existing. We have already discussed this in committee. The Minister of Sports has said that it is not the sportsmen's fault – I share that – that it is not the coach's fault either. Then you yourself, I think, have said that the structures are a problem. If you look at the draft of this sports promotion law, you can see that it is in front of you that it is built on the existing structures. So if the structures are a problem, then you probably have to change them and can't build on them. That is the purpose of this law. My thesis is – and we will find it difficult to clarify today because we have to look at the effect of the law – that very little will change with this form, as the law is designed. We have a decision-making structure where in the bodies – and we do not need to describe this in detail – a sports fund, a funding fund is ultimately set up, where decisions are made, with the BSO having the absolute majority; the BSO representatives even have a two-thirds majority. The BSO represents the whole range of sports, and if you want to become BSO President, like Mr Wittmann, you will need the votes of the entire BSO and you will be able to afford it badly to go to a certain focus here. My assessment is: It has changed a lot in control – I admit that was also one of the essential demands – and the number of controls has been improved. If you go into detail – even this was a debate in the committee that we couldn't really carry out, because then you said that you didn't agree – if you look more closely at how the control of content looks, then you can see that it gets very thin. While there is a requirement to apply for grants, there is only one part as a basic structure – okay, useful measure that essentially needs to be written for the other part – but if these funding objectives, which one must also write in, are not fulfilled, there is also the need to explain why they have not been implemented and which structural measures are being taken. So my prognosis is: It will change relatively little. We will see in the next few years whether or not the structures, the funding criteria, are changing. I would assume that associations that simply descend over time also have to suffer corresponding consequences. If you look at the Austrian swimming association – I say this here in this form too – you see, there has been a lot wrong in recent years: away from one of the associations that had a certain width, that de facto almost all the top athletes have stopped. In the support structure, there is now relatively little change and little action. So: If you want to change this in top sports, you should have put it there. Second point: In my opinion, the mistake in Austrian sport is the lack of connection between school and organized sport. There is also something inside, there are small possibilities of promotion inside. But as long as there is no massive cooperation between the Ministry of Education and the Ministry of Sports, we will not solve the problem. The point is that the Austrian sports infrastructure – and I am now talking about those places where it is most present, namely in schools – is exactly locked at the time when the children would have the opportunity to use it, namely during their holidays. There you can see that we have a massive use problem and a massive problem with sports. If it is not possible to open the schools in the holidays – especially in summer holidays, but in the holidays in general – to make offers there, then long-distance sports in Austria will have a great difficulty. And there you have to say quite objectively: Even in a situation in which, for some time now, the Ministry of Sport and the Ministry of Education have been in the same political design, both at the SPÖ, it has not succeeded in overcoming these structural obstacles. This remains the core problem, the debate has always gone the same way: The Ministry of Sports says that the schools do that; the Ministry of Education says that is the sport – and that does not make it possible to connect. The connection between broadband and top sports is relatively simple. When does broadband come into being? – If there are successes in the top sports in these sports. There was also a tennis boom in Austria, in the mid-1990s. It is relatively ineffective, which must also be said in terms of top-level sport, now also in terms of broadband. If you play tennis and look at the halls, you will find that the halls are increasingly locked up. In Tattendorf we now have a dog service in the tennis hall, other tennis halls go together. What really is missing is that where success occurs in the top sport, it can be responded relatively quickly and offers are created. That's where young people come, and they don't wait three years after patterns have won in Paris for them to take control of the tennis racket. You'll be there two weeks later, and you'll have to be able to make an offer to them. And I wonder where this flexibility should be in here. As a result, there are improvements in control, but many things have not really been done. Finally, I would like to say that it might have been possible to create at least certain spaces here. I say one word: Inclusion of Austrian sportsids. There has been some criticism, for example, of Mr Patterne, not only of the table tennis, but also of a massive criticism of the organisation's structures. Mr Seisenbacher – that is what we discussed at the time in the Sports Committee, whether we can afford it; it may be that at some point one could have afforded it. We don't have so many Olympic winners in Austria; it wouldn't be that they're inside. If they want to do something for Austrian sport, then the same thing happens as in the past: You can go to the association and you can try to move something in the association structure. The only problem is that exactly those who say in part that in the association structure they could never have achieved the successes. If you don't make it so that you give them the opportunity to get involved, and everyone refers only to the associations, then we will have the difficulty of bringing the idol that exists in Austria, also into the sport. So I wish my prognosis wasn't right, but that something really changed; it's different, unfortunately. We will see the evaluation in a few years – let us say so – and it will not take that long. The hope is admittedly low, but perhaps it will also succeed in bringing about changes in the future, that at least certain priorities will be made possible, which are not in the classical, organized form, but rather new, innovative opportunities and which really provide access to sports that creates a wide range in Austria. Thank you.

It has already been mentioned, that has been since 1948, i.e. since the post-war period, that can be claimed with fug and law, the largest reform in the area of federal sports promotion. We know that we have combined several problems in the situation in the field of sport promotion. There is a lack of transparency. There is a control that includes deficits. There is support according to the principle of foundry. There are many funding pots that work together. The administrative burden is high, and the planning security for the associations is not very high. With the new law, we will make significant progress in this context, essentially an increase in efficiency, and in addition to significantly professionalising the existing support system. The new model significantly improves association promotions, and it brings considerable, clear and clear separation in relation to broadband and top-notch sports, in relation to 50 : 45 : 5. The top sport benefits significantly from this federal sports promotion reform, with around €5 million more per year. We will also succeed in this context – I will later come to speak of my teaching, which I have drawn from the Olympic Games – I am firmly convinced with this sport promotion to make a significant positive contribution to red-and-white-red sports, and thus also a contribution to getting to the models in Austria in the medium and long term, to role models for our children and young people who can focus on it in order to revitalize them for sports. However, it is also important to me that the space between stricter control of content on the one hand and administrative simplifications, simplified accounting systems on the other side are handled. ParlaMint-AT_2013-05-23-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00204_d7e24957	Next speaker: Mr Gahr. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-05-23-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00204_d7e24978	Madam President. Mr President of the Court of Auditors! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) My previous speaker already presented it: It is about the examination of the Austrian Institute of Technology, formerly Austrian Research Center, all known to us under the research center Seibersdorf. There is a moving past here, and the Court of Auditors has carried out two audits, one on the one hand, and the other on the other, on the other hand, on the other hand a follow-up review. There have been serious shortcomings and mismanagements. But this Court of Auditors report also shows us that it is possible to lead a state-based company from the loss zone to the profit zone through streamlining and reform. Since 2010, the company has been re-established. There were four main criticism points, on the one hand the in-sich business. There was a very complex structure and many links, and there were no guidelines for these in-sich-businesses. On the other hand, the funding was improperly used. There were also subsidies that were previously rejected and approved in retrospect. Another point was that there was no written service contracts and, on the other hand, the salaries were extremely lucrative, where only a few employees were responsible. The sale of the FEG, functional materials Research and Development GmbH, was also not carried out according to commercial principles. 23 recommendations were made by the Court of Auditors. Of these, 19 were completely unscathed and four were partially unscathed. The main measures were: Recast of the shareholder contract according to the stencil regulation of the federal government, development of an overall corporate strategy and reduction of business areas, significant savings in personnel costs, introduction of a department controlling, introduction of an internal revision, establishment of a research marketing and conclusion of periodic performance agreement between BMVIT and AIT. Today, the AIT is a showcase institution, which claims to be in competition and exists in competition. AIT is the largest non-university research institution in Austria. In 2011, a positive profit of € 6.1 million was achieved. A result of the audit of the Court of Auditors was that almost one million could be recovered from the Ministry of Transport, where subsidies were unjustly received. Overall, this Court of Auditors report is a contribution and a mission to lead an important company into a good and successful future. The Court of Auditors and its reform measures have succeeded in rethinking and getting out of the headlines. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2013-05-23-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00204_d7e27348	Mr President. High house! On the agenda today there are 15 Court of Auditors reports, a request review, four cross-sectional audits, four main audits, a sample examination, five follow-up checks. Therefore, allow me to make a few brief comments, including with regard to the debate that has been held: The effectiveness of the Court of Auditors has been addressed: The follow-up reviews on the agenda are certainly giving a good witness to this. Of 81 recommendations from the Court of Auditors, 69 recommendations have been implemented or are in implementation. 12 recommendations are open. Considering the effectiveness of the Court of Auditors also in figures, this shows only the examination of transport telematics, where the audit of the Court of Auditors has led to detectable savings of € 192 million, because the Court of Auditors' recommendation in this case redimensional transport telematics projects, or at the same time the investment programme has been reduced, without in any way restricting the functioning of the Court of Auditors. In addition, the Court of Auditors has repeatedly referred to examinations which give you the opportunity to draw the necessary conclusions. In this context, I refer to the health and care tests. If today’s review of the “financing of benefits in hospitals and ordinations” is on the agenda, it shows that the effects or objectives of the health reforms have not yet been implemented and that relocation, namely from the stationary area in the outpatient area or in the settled area, has not been carried out because the necessary incentives are not given. That is, it pays off to take up a patient in a hospital, because the hospital receives such additional income. It pays off not to investigate a patient in the hospital, but to release him so that he has to carry out his preliminary studies there and then only have to be operated on in the hospital; this is called preoperative diagnostics, while saving one at the expense of the other. These examples show that the patient has to adapt to the structures and do not have to address the structures to the patient. The same takes place in the care of the elderly: In old-age care, we have an Article 15a agreement from 1993, which provides for the same benefits as minimum standards under the same conditions. However, if you look at the old-age care we have tested, you will see considerable differences in the costs per home, the requirements for care staff, the structural design of the homes, the provision of homes. Have alone in Tyrol She's the one who's got it. She's the one who's got it. 391 different tariffs, and with the same level of care and the same care effort, a price difference of over 900 €. In addition, if you look at the financial flows, complete transparency exists: To finance a home area of €3 200 you need 19 financing flows. How does that look? Just a small example because time has advanced. The federal government pays the money, for example a pension insurance institution. The pension insurance institution pays the money to the home residents, who pays the home operator, for example to a community association the home rate. The municipality pays the fee to the country. The country finances investments and gives money plus sales tax back to the association. At the same time, the Federal Government grants the country a promotion of the same amount as the sales tax according to the Health and Social Aid Act (in this case). This is a way, marked by transparency, which certainly belongs to eliminated and where it turns out that measures should be taken. In addition, there is potential for optimization in the field of research. This shows, for example, the MedAustron audit, and this shows the audit that has been addressed several times today, the FWG, for example, in which more than € 2 million of funding money has been used and in the end the company was sold by € 50 000. The Court of Auditors has examined all the documents which have been made available to it and which it has found in response to its work. The result is also slender in the area. It has also been pointed out what the honourable Member Graf said in this context that it was originally tried to merge the company, that a successful company was sold by €50 000, and after it was sold by €50 000, shortly thereafter the managing director in the FWG or the commercial manager of the ARC, with the participation of the managing director in the case amounting to €300 000 in the case. That is, if you calculate it about highly, that the company has won €3 million in value within a short time, even though it has been sold by €50,000. It is true that Mr Kogler has said that the Court of Auditors has no duty of truth, that the Court of Auditors cannot examine documents that are not present and that there is no obligation to submit them. This is certainly a fact to be considered and to point out, for example, that a false statement of evidence in court according to § 288 StGB and before an administrative authority according to § 289 StGB is punishable. A circumstance to think about, also with regard to Salzburg, where this has been addressed. In addition, there is also a need for action when you look at the film promotion, with 17 different funding institutions at the federal and national level. 65 percent of the funding is provided by more than one body without adequate coordination and without the corresponding objectives being specified. That is, there is a need for action. The need for action to be taken by the Court of Auditors' audits. However, it is also right to say that the Court of Auditors must also ensure that funds are used effectively and effectively. You can see here a table with staff costs since 2007 to 2013. [[The speaker shall keep a document up to date.]] The lower line is the increase in staff costs of the Court of Auditors. If you look at the Court’s output, you will see that the output has increased from 2005 to 2012. If the Court of Auditors had published 3 000 pages in 2005, it was more than five times as many in 2012, more than 16,000 pages. Just to mention a digit. In addition, however, it should also be noted – as this has also been mentioned – that a large number of administrative tasks have been entrusted to the Court of Auditors: We have the Party Act, the Media Transparency Act, but at the same time we have a budget which is much higher in 2012 than in 2013, 2014, 2015 and 2016, although additional tasks must be achieved, annual increase in references must be covered, structural effects covered and annual price increases should also be added to the actual effort. More administrative tasks, less money means less at the same time. Therefore, I need to inform you about this: They are the first recipients of the audit of the Court of Auditors. They see the impact of the Court of Auditors, which means that it is appropriate to have audits, to strengthen transparency and accountability. It is therefore up to you to draw the necessary conclusions from it. As in the past, the Court of Auditors will certainly do its utmost. Thank you.
